Myanmar King Alaungpaya’s
Invasion
Alaungpaya was born in 1714 at shwebo,
a village in north Myanmar. It was later
known as Moksobommyo, meaning the
‘city of hunter chief.’ Aungzeya was son
of the head man of the village. In 1740
the Mon king of Pegu bearing the title
of Smin Htaw Buddhaketi attacked the
Myanmar kingdom of Ava. He was
defeated by Banya Dala (1747-57)
Aungzeya had attacked the Mons in 1753
and forced Banya Dala to make a retreat
from Ava in the following year. He then
marched south in 1755 and seized Dagon
and renamed it Yangon meaning ‘end of
the enemy.’ Aungzeya then took the title
of Alaungpaya went back to upper
Myanmar and reinforced his army. In
May 1757 Alaungpaya, surrounded Pegu
and finally occupied the Mon capital.
The Mon king Banya Dala and his family
were all seized. The famous Mon monk-
writer Acwo’ of Wawmyo a town near
Pegu, stated that Alaungpaya was the
most cruel king who even put to death
over three thousand Mon monks and
destroyed almost all of the Mon
manuscripts and stone inscriptions. He
even changed the ethnic name of Mon
to ‘Talaing,’ by which the Mon people
hated to be called up to the present day.
During his taking over Pegu, numerous
Mon people fled to Thailand for safety.
In 1760 Alaungpaya came and attacked
Ayutthaya, but he did not succeed due to
the rainy season. Alaungpaya withdrew
and died on the way at the foot of Mt.
kelasa. (Dr. Nai Pan Hla-Eleven Mon
Dhammasat texts. 1992, page-xxvi)
Another reason is some Mon do not
know to preserve their higher generation
or position and they do not take pride
their higher generation, some Mon want
to save the money and they think that
Kana dance do the extra works. So they
demoted themselves from higher
generation to lower generation and offer
the ancestor worship on Sitting Position
from the Kana dance position, They did

not know really that they reduced the
Status of their generation and position.
How to build the Kana
According to the survey in Kaw
Bein village, Kawkharait township
Karen state Myanmar, we knew that 6
poles are used to hold on ground when
dun build the Kana. The length of poles
are 10-hot (10-cubits) (1-hot=18-
inches), hold 3-poles on one each side
and the distant between the two poles are
5-hot. The roof is covered local made
leave. The length of roof is 3-kwin
(kwin-sheet). One kwin in length is 4-
hot and one hand span (1-hand span-9-
inches). The main building roof length
is 4-hot and the outside roof length is 3-
hot. Under the Kana in east side, it is built
the shelf. The wide of shelf is 4-hot to
put the material for worship on it. The
height of shelf is height of kana owner’s
chest. Round on the inner roof hang the
32 flowers. On the inner are people tied
with coconut flowers. Betel flowers,
spring of Eugenia, long blanket and long
ancestor wear cloth. The building of
Kana gate is opened in the west, 10-feet
far from the gate they plant the two
Eugenia trees, under one tree they make
the fire and boiling a pot of water until
the end of the Kana dance. Mostly, the
relation who descended from the
generation or ancestor can enter, play and
dance under Kana the others are
prohibited to enter the Kana.
The original of Kana dancing
According to the Mon history the
last of Mon king was defeaded by
Myanmar king and Mon people were
genocided by Myanmar army. So, Mon
people fled to the save place and some
migrated into Thailand. In the peaceful
time, they remembered their king, their
nation, their culture and tradition. Kana
dance was played both sides of Mon in
Myanmar and Mon in Thailand. Mostly,
the items dancing of the Kana are their
memorial and fulfill their mind.
1. Leh Takiup Kana the main person
of clan leads dance and goes round the
Kana and he dances with two swords in
both hands. The other clans, one holds a
white umbrella, one holds a pennant and
the left hold the flowers follow with him
by dancing. Sometime they call this item
patiunjan.
This item is taken part in every
Kana dance, describing to establish the
nation or the king attends the throne. The
chief clan leads dancing as like as the
king holds the two swords in his two
186

[page 191]
SUC ILM nn eee SAG INO WF lee Ta: cal
hands, one person covers him with white
umbrella and follows behind him. The
crown prince and royal family follow as
like the royal occasion and round the
palace three times. It represents that the
king and his followers round the palace
and the country to take care the people.
2.  Baliup Tomkalok It isthe cerem-
onial of bathing the main person of clan.
In every Kana dance, this ceremonial was
taken part as an item of dancing. There
are two kinds, bathing the offering and
bathing the stem. Ii means as like as a
king. Before attending with throne, the
king washs the feet, hands and has a bath.
The intention of this dance is to this
dance is to memorize the Mon king. And
then in the Kana dance, they called Bca
tala pion, means to offer the food to the
king as the main sept. -
3. Bhat Tnambhrat It is the cerem-
onial of cutting the banana plant. Dun is
dancing with a banana plant and the
person of main clan also dances with a
sword and he cut the roof of banana plant
first and then cut into three parts of
banana plant. Every kana dance contains
this item. It means the king conquered
the enemy and caught the chief of enemy
who was killed in the ceremony. The king
was standing, on the high shelf, one of
his men was pouring the water on his feet
for washing, while the washed water of
feet were falling down in the prey, the
executioner cut the head first and cut the
body into three parts.
‘the great king Rajardhirat stepped at the
face of Prince Min Ye Kyaw Sway and
climbed up on the shelf. The king was
waiting the coincided in time for a great
successful lunar mansion poured the
water on the king’s feet and washed the
feet. While the washed water was falling
down on to the Prince Min Ye Kyaw
Sway who was lost his life by the
executioners. The parohites (Domestic
chaplain of a king) bathed the water from
conch sheil to the king and the king
stayed under the golden Pja’tha (spire-
like roof) with ministers and parohites.”
(Ref;-A Kriun Rajardhirat- Compiled by
Nai Maung Toe, 2000. Pg-255)
4. Minorities dancing In old Mon

S. Indian civilization influenced on
Mon Mons believe deeply in Buddh-
ism. But before they had met the Lord
Buddha and learnt the teaching of
Buddha, they did not know definitely
what they believe while they were settl-

and literature. There are two epics of

Southeast-Asia people.

golden deer in Yamayana drama. But
now most of dancers, duns and owners
of the Kana in kana do not know the

6. Mon historical essential events
influence on Kana dance.
The Mon hero was prince Asahk-
umar who was brought up among the
buffaloes in the forest. Prince Asah was
son of king Samala and queen Suvanna.
His uncle Wimala killed his father before
he come to the world and made his
mother be the chief queen who was
pregnant. When the prince was born, his
uncle ordered the queen to put away the
child. Queen Suvanna took her baby out
into the forest and hid him there. A
female buffalo Nang Glaung pitied the
child and performed as a foster-mother
of the prince. As prince Asahkumar was
brought up among the buffaloes, he was
strong and clever in riding and playing
on the buffaloes. When prince Asah-
kumar was 16 year old, he was discov-
ered by a hunter and coincidently the
Mon king-Wimala was searching for a
Mon hero to fight against the Indian’s
challenge. Due to the victory, king
Wimala made his nephew Asahkumar as
his successor. Asahkumar was then
known as Smoin Asah as the 3 king of
Pegu. Smoin Asah founded the pagoda
1 Sa a , SAGINOWF
SIE EMT ES SEL
on the eastern side of the mouth of
Sittaung River which later called
Kyaikkatah in Myanmar and kyaik Asah
in Mon. this Kyaikkatha site yields
numerious silver coins and votive tablets
dating to 6" century A.D (Nai Pan Hla-
The Significant role of the Mon language
and culture in Southeast Asia. Pg.54.
1992)
One day the king played an irritated
game with female dewa who was looks
after the Sittaung River. She was angry
as the buffaloes always crossed the ae
from west side to east side bank to eat
the grass every day. Sittaung River was
dirty because of them. The bet was that
the loser had to fulfill the winner’s desire.
Unluckily, the King Asah lost in game.
She wanted a buffalo which had the
golden horn. Among the real golden horn
buffaloes, the foster-mother buffalo
Nang Glaung was the only one buffaloes
which had real golden horns. The foster
mother buffalo was killed by the king’s
promise. The king was deeply sorry to
his whole life, for the sake of his gamble
his foster mother buffalo died. Mon
people were also sad and angry to the
female dewa of Sittaung River and the
tortoise ridden by female dewa when she
came to the game and helped to win un-
fair game. Annually, the king ordered his
189

[page 194]
OPES RELA ELT
people to make a big Kana and offered
to his mother buffalo. A Mon poem on
the story of Asahkumar is very romantic.
Mon started to offer the ancestor worship
in the Kana from that time surely.
These historical facts are taken part in
the Kana dance. In every Mon Kana
dance, we can see the role of eating
tortoise, eating the grilled fish, eating a
snakehead fish was made a whole in the
septum of the nose as like as buffalo.
Instead of buffalo they used the
snakehead fish, both buffalo and
snakehead fish stay in mud. Even so
called Myanmar who live both in Yangon
and Pegu Division they offer the Nang
Glaung buffalo, the statue of an old lady
wear the buffalo head and is placed in
front of their house. These people are
descended from Mon generation. But
they cannot speak in Mon and they do
not know they are Mon generation but
they are living on the ancient Mon land.
So they believe the Mon faith. According
to the historical facts, Mon believe that
tortoise is the worst creature in all. If a
Mon catches a tortoise, he must cook it
and offer to the ancestor worship. If he
does not kill, he must throw that tortoise
as if it is rotten and smelly.
ME CEASE CL
7. Representative their task and
their surrounding play in Kana.
Most Mon are farmers and live in the
villages which are near the farm, river,
sea, and forest. Their companions are
oxen, buffaloes and elephants. In ancient
time elephant was very important in their
life and in the history. Especially. the king
who was the ownership of white
elephants was very famous in history.
The group of well trained elephant was
very important in the battle field. So,
Mon were always finding the elephant
in the forest, especially to catch the white
elephant for king. They trapped the
elephant in the forest and trained in the
field to be tame. According to this event,
in Kana dance catching and training the
elephant is one part of playing the dance,
Such as Mons are living near the sea and
river, thus the boat is very important in
their life. Boat is useful to carry the
passengers, load the goods. battle in the
navy and marine field that was essential
in Mon history. According to these facts,
in Kana dance they play the boat dance.
The boat dance is the last item. The boat
is made of banana plant and all food used
in Kana are put into the boat and the dun
recite so long that all evil spirits drown
in the deep sea, dun threw the boat
outside so from the kana.
eo

[page 195]
One of the main work and in-come
of Mon is to weave cloth. Most of old
Mon ladies can weave on loom. After
have finished their farm the ladies weave
on loom under the house. Most of Mon
house are high poles. Men live in up floor
and loom, cart and cattle are stored in
ground floor. The ladies weave the cloth
for their families. According to this main
task, in the kana dun dances with two
sticks these are hollow in the middle as
like as the loom. The ancestor’s clothes
in the kana are weaved by themselves.
8. Traditional game contains in
Kana.
In the Kana dance we can see the
dance of Minblay Bhgna- the young
famer, playing to throw the birds with
bow, playing the top, playing with doll,
fighting the cooks and playing the Entada
round seeds. These represent the nature
of Mon youth.
Now a day Mon in Thailand, at
Sangkram (New-year) festival, they
throw water to each other and get use to
visiting each other’s houses and hold
parties to drink homemade rice wine. A
very popular game which was played
among boys and girls especially at night
during new-year festival is called
winglee. This game is played in groups
where the Entada round seed about
3inches in circumference are set upright
in a long at which each boy and girl from
a distance of about ten to twenty feet
would throw with the same seed that each
of them holds in hand to knock down
the seeds set on the row. It is a traditional
form of courtship at which the boys and
girls could easily get acquaintance with
each other. But between Mon youths at
Mon state in Myanmar, this game is
disappeared for a long time.
9. Drive out the evil sprit harai or
ghost contains in Kana dance.
Bet harai or thaden harai is driven evil
spirit or ghost from the person who is
unwell not because of these. In the kana
dance unwell person sits in Kana by
laying out his or her legs, the dun is
holding two swords and performs as he
rubs with swords from head to legs of
sitting person by dancing with music.
While the night has lunar eclipses the
villagers beat the tin and hollowed log
which means to drive out the ghost and
evil spirit from house and villages is one
of the traditional in Mon village. In kana
dance they call taleh-beating the Ioo.
While one group of men and children fine
beating the long log or pole, the dun and
his followers dance and feed them the
19]

[page 196]
mixture of fish or tortoise with salad.
In the Kana dance, they call tak
khapuo or kalok.khapuo which means
touch limestone to clan person of Kana
to prevent from evil spirits. The dun
chews a quid of betel with more
limestone and dance with music and then
she blow betel liquid to the head of one
lady of clan. Today in Thailand not only
in Mon house but also in Thai house, we
can see three spots of limestone on the
front doors. After the monks had recited
the Suta which forwards off evil or harm,
they touch three limestone liquid spots
at the front enter door with brush.
10. Offering the local spirit
Every Kana dance of Mon in
Myanmar contains dance of bhau kaw
yan and Bhau Yan Ngem which means
dancing for main spirit of Kyaw Yan
town and Yan ngan town. These towns
were contained in the Martaban 32
Mandala and famous in olden day in
Mon history. Now it remains only as a
big village and is called in Myanmar Yin
Nyein that locates on the rail road from
Thaton to Martaban. According to the
place they live in and the desire of owners
of Kana dance, they offer to chief spirit
of local area with dance.
11. According to the owner promises,
they have to dance the Kana-paleh
galan.
Paleh means play or dance, galan
means promises of talking. The chief of
clan or his generation give promise to
the ancestor to play the Kana dance. They
give promise that they will give next
kana in every next five year or one
decade. Some also give promise. If they
become rich or heaithy or get successful
lives, they will pay Kana dance before
due date.
This item is mostly dance in the first
part. The stem talks to his ancestor that
he pays the kana dance according to his
promises. The sun leads him to dance
with Mon traditional dance of 12 kinds-
Leh cehba ‘akhiuk. According to our
survey in May 2008 at Zimkyaik village
in Pound town-ship Mon state,
Myanmar. The 12 kinds-leh cehba
akhiuk- of Mon traditional dances are:-
1.Paleh galan gata First dancing accor-
ding to promises.
2.Paleh galan lakrau Last
according to promises.

3.Poy bhabin Dancing with the sound of
big drum.

But these twelve kinds of Kana
dance are very different from twelve
kinds of Mon traditional dance.
Twelve Style of Mon Dancing or
Choreography
1. Leh kreoa Ca sat Kren It means
‘the dance of the crane picks up the fruit
of eugenia’ these dance adopted the
nature of bird, flying into the tree and
flying out from the tree, resting on the
banch, picking the fruit, pushing each
other, flying in circles.
2.Lek Lyat Pnik It means ‘the dance
held instrument with either a broad
ridged surface or with a semicircular
surface which can be opened or closed
on a pivot. The dancers moved their
hands, head and body as like as the fan
movement.
3. Lek Pkao Dkah It means ‘the dance
as like as lotus’. The lotus the design of
lotus bud, blossom, bloom and petal on
the sun shine were adopted by the Mon
dancers. So, the dancer’s head, hands and
body movement are as likely as a lotus
bud, bloom and petal.
4. Lek Kon Know It means ‘the dance
of Kinnari’. Kinnari was female and
Kinnar was male. They were mythical
being half-human and half-bird mixture
body like an angel in western legend.
Kinnari-kinnara story was spread widely
in the legend of Mon and Southeast Asia
people, especially in Myanmar Thailand
and Cambodia. In the legend, they were
musicians and loved the music love to
sing and dance and love each other very
much more than human being loved each
other. According to this legend, they are
very beautiful and attractive. The design
of their fly, wave the wings, pick the food
and feed to each other could attracted to
dancers. Mon dancers imitated the way
of their movement and dance like their

[page 198]
designs. This legend was written more
than two hundred years ago in palm leave
and in old Mon manuscript.
5S. Lek Cen Dow It means ‘The dance of
jungle fowl’. Finding food together is the
nature of fowl. Both male and female
fowl rake to find the food. When male
fowl get food, he calls his female fowl.
As a vice versa the female fowl acts like
male fowl. When male fowl proposes for
love to female fowl, he opens the wings
and circles to her. This movement was
adopted by the Mon dancer naturally and
lovely. This style of dance is mostly used
in the romantic dance by male and female
dancer called ‘Poy Sma Dwk’.
6. Lek Dun Giun It meanes ‘The dance
of rowing boat’. In early time, Mon
coutry was in lower Burma. That region
was heavy rain and had so many rivers,
creeks and streams. Mon used the river
and water ways with boats and rafts. So,
they were skillful in rowing the boats and
water ways. The festival of rowing boat
was held when the king and royal family
were wandering the country by river way
in olden day. But tody only two reasons
are left to hold rowing boat festivals. One
is to carry the oldest monk or to donate
the properties for monks from one village
to another village by boat and the crews
sing the song while they are rowing.
Another is in the month of Bhat or Kanya
(September), Mon village are sited on the
river bank those villages make the
festival of rowing the boat. The designs
of the boat’s crews rowing are adopted
by the dancer.
7. Lek Wen Pkao It meanes
‘The dance of holding flowers in two
hands’. This dance has been descended
from the dance of festival to honour the
spirit-Mon called Kna dance. The dance
is following the song recounting the story
of the previous existence of the spirit.
Sometimes the dance is following the
special music for spirit played at the time
of propitiation at spirits. The dancers
hold the bunch of flowers with both
hands and hold up the two hands. lean
the body and choral dancing. According
to the music of spirit, sometimes instead
of bunch of flowers, the dancers hold two
poles and dance as they dance with the
flowers. Sometimes the dancers hold two
swords and dance as they dance with the
flowers. Although the holding materials
are changed the design the dances are
same.
8. Lek Lpuh Toa Dewataolt means
‘The dance is as like as Dawata point out
his forefinger’. The main design of this
dance is pointing out the forefinger to
ten directions and dance according to the
— 194 ——

[page 199]
song and music.
9. Lek Dun Bmak_ The meaning of the
Dun Bmak is eastern country. The eastern
country of Mon is Thailand. The design
of this dance is as like as the dancing of
Thai dancers. The design of dance is
similar to the dance of fan. This dancing
design is only dance with song composed
by Mna group method.
10. Lek Wen Jran It means ‘Dance
with swords’ The dancer holds the two
swords tightly in each hand and dance
as the fighting with swords. This design
of dancing is very exciting to the
audiences. Sometime they named it the
national spirit dance or the General
fighter dance.
11. lek Wen Pkao , It means ‘It is a
dancing of offering flowers to Lord
Buddha. The dancers demonstrated how
to pick up the flowers in the forest, carry,
wash, make the wreath and offer the Lord
Buddha. When the audiences watch that
design of dance, they feel to donate the
things, to offer and merit to Lord Buddha.
12. Poy Pa Leh Ha Lan It is performed
both in spiritual and single dance.
When the high tune is played, the
dancer dances energetically. When the
low tune is played, he dances smoothly.
Mostly, the Bat Kiun musical
instrument brass gongs hanged on the
sun or moon shade box is used in that
dance. This design of dance can be seen
in the festival of spiritual dance in the
villages.

AN ALARMING GLOBAL PROBLEM,
DEPRESSION IN CHILDREN AND ADOLESCENTS
With the development of stre- ssful
lifestyle nowadays, a mental problem,
depression come into one of the most
popular mental illness resulted all over the
world.These problems affect adults and
elderlys as well as children and adoles-
cents.
= (0°

[page 201]
Te =F IIS EEE SLT a
Today,may I talk about depres-
sion in the latter group .Research
states depression affects at least 2%
of children under 12 and 5%of teen-
agers, much going unrecognized. It
often presents with somatic, anxiety,
or poor functioning at school, soci-
ety, or at home, even masquarading
as bad beheaviour particularly in
boys. Mood is characteristically uch
more variable and less pervasive than
adults rapid swings commonly occur.
The fact that children are able to en-
joy some aspects of their life does not
preclude depression.
Features as seen in adults:
- Low mood
- Loss of interest
- Socially withdrawn
- Poor self-esteem
- Psychomotor retardation
- Fearfulness
- Feeling of guilt
- Anxiety
- Lack of enjoyment in anything .
Features common in childhood:
- Running away from home
- Separation anxiety with or
without
school refusal
- Complaints of boredom
- Poor school performance
- Antisocial beheaviour
-Insomnia(often initial,not early wak-
ing) -
Hypersomnia
-Eating problems.
Young children may present with
sadness and helplessness:slightly
older children with feeling of being
unloved and unfairly treated;whilst
guilt and despair may be more promi-
nent in teenagers.
Interviewing is best conducted
one-to-one.Consider possibility of
concealed factors (eg.past child
abuse, bullying).
Parents can be interviewed separately
(ask the child’s permission, and see
the child alone, again, if appropriate,
after seeing the parents,to report
back:this helps avoid the impression
that you are somehow colluding with
the parents). NB:parents may not al-
ways be aware of depression in their
children. Assessment is often difficult:
questions may only be answered by
silence or a shrug. If not getting any-
where, talk to the child_ or just offer
silences but do not give up.
Always ask about thoughts of
suicide and self-harm. Any past at-
tempts (increased risk if: conduct dis-
order, increased alcohol use, help-
lessness, or in local authority care),
Self-harm may be a form of commu-
nication_ a message in bottle: not
always picked up’ and sometimes it
197

[page 202]
ime
is difficult to deciper the teenagers in-
tentions. Refer urgently if risk is sig-
nificant. Adolescent with conduct
disorders can be manipulative and ex-
tremely difficult to assess_an urgent
second opinion is frequently of help.
How to manage this problem:
Ideally this should be a combined ap-
proach_ with the involvement of the
child or adolescent,his or her
parents,social workers and doctors.
Ls Social
interventions_addressing any sources
of distress (eg.bullying) and remov-
ing opportunities for self-harm
(eg.paracetamol at home).
2. Psychological interventions
encourage verbalizing of moods; ex-
plore the vocabulary of internal
states. Councellers, good teachers,and
youth workers may be suitable
resources.Cognitive-beheavioural
therapy has been shown to be of
help_but may not be freely available
in primary care. a.
Drugs:often disappointing and
dangerous.But as option 1 and 2 above
often give suboptimal results,drugs
need to be considered.
- Evaluate risk and benefit ratio
(try to
include parents in the discussion)
- Monitor suicide ideation more

weekly for the first month,and then
every other weekly.
- Before an antidepressant is initiated,
a
safety plan should be in place with
an
agreement with the patient and
the family the patient will be kept
safe and
will contact a responsible adult
if
suicidal urges get too strong.
- Ensure the availability of psychi-
